=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N houses at points (X_1,Y_1),\ldots,(X_N,Y_N) on a two-dimensional plane.
Initially, Santa Claus is at point (S_x,S_y). He will act according to the sequence (D_1,C_1),\ldots,(D_M,C_M) as follows:

- For i=1,2,\ldots,M in order, he moves as follows:
- Let (x,y) be the point where he currently is.
- If D_i is U, move in a straight line from (x,y) to (x,y+C_i).
- If D_i is D, move in a straight line from (x,y) to (x,y-C_i).
- If D_i is L, move in a straight line from (x,y) to (x-C_i,y).
- If D_i is R, move in a straight line from (x,y) to (x+C_i,y).





Find the point where he is after completing all actions, and the number of distinct houses he passed through or arrived at during his actions. If the same house is passed multiple times, it is only counted once.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M S_x S_y
X_1 Y_1
\vdots
X_N Y_N
D_1 C_1
\vdots
D_M C_M

Output

Let (X,Y) be the point where he is after completing all actions, and C be the number of distinct houses passed through or arrived at. Print X,Y,C in this order separated by spaces.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2\times 10^5
- 1 \leq M \leq 2\times 10^5
- -10^9 \leq X_i,Y_i \leq 10^9
- The pairs (X_i,Y_i) are distinct.
- -10^9 \leq S_x,S_y \leq 10^9
- There is no house at (S_x,S_y).
- Each D_i is one of U, D, L, R.
- 1 \leq C_i \leq 10^9
- All input numbers are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 4 3 2
2 2
3 3
2 1
L 2
D 1
R 1
U 2

Sample Output 1

2 3 2

Santa Claus behaves as follows:


- D_1= L, so he moves from (3,2) to (3-2,2) in a straight line. During this, he passes through the house at (2,2).
- D_2= D, so he moves from (1,2) to (1,2-1) in a straight line.
- D_3= R, so he moves from (1,1) to (1+1,1) in a straight line. During this, he passes through the house at (2,1).
- D_4= U, so he moves from (2,1) to (2,1+2) in a straight line. During this, he passes through the house at (2,2), but it has already been passed.

The number of houses he passed or arrived during his actions is 2.

Sample Input 2

1 3 0 0
1 1
R 1000000000
R 1000000000
R 1000000000

Sample Output 2

3000000000 0 0

Be careful with overflow.

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

import sys, bisect
from collections import defaultdict

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it)); M = int(next(it))
    Sx = int(next(it)); Sy = int(next(it))
    houses = []
    for _ in range(N):
        x = int(next(it)); y = int(next(it))
        houses.append((x,y))
    # group houses by x? We'll sort later.
    events = defaultdict(list)  # x -> list of (delta, y)
    vertical = defaultdict(list)  # x -> list of (y1,y2)
    x = Sx; y = Sy
    for _ in range(M):
        d = next(it).decode()? Actually bytes. D_i token like b'U'. We can compare to b'U' etc.
        c = int(next(it))
        if d == b'U':
            y2 = y + c
            vertical[x].append((y, y2))  # y < y2
            y = y2
        elif d == b'D':
            y2 = y - c
            vertical[x].append((y2, y))
            y = y2
        elif d == b'L':
            x2 = x - c
            # horizontal y from x2 to x
            events[x2].append((1, y))  # add at left
            events[x+1].append((-1, y)) # remove after right? Wait right endpoint is x (current before move), left x2. Segment covers [x2, x]. Add at x2, remove at x+1. Yes.
            x = x2
        else: # R
            x2 = x + c
            events[x].append((1, y))
            events[x2+1].append((-1, y))
            x = x2
    # Need ensure for L, if x2 < x, add at x2, remove at x+1. But if x+1 maybe? yes. For R, add at x, remove at x2+1.
    # For vertical D, y2 < y, interval (y2,y). For U, (y,y2).
    # But note for vertical, if multiple intervals at same x, merge later.

    # Preprocess vertical intervals
    vertical_merged = {}  # x -> (starts, ends)
    for vx, intervals in vertical.items():
        intervals.sort()
        starts = []
        ends = []
        for l,r in intervals:
            if not starts or l > ends[-1]: # no overlap
                starts.append(l); ends.append(r)
            else:
                if r > ends[-1]: ends[-1] = r
        vertical_merged[vx] = (starts, ends)

    # Sort houses by x
    houses.sort(key=lambda p: p[0])
    # Sort event keys and house x? Need include house x. We can build keys = set(events.keys()) then add houses x. But events keys maybe huge. We can iterate over sorted(events.keys() | house_xs). But set union. Use keys = set(events.keys()); for hx,hy in houses: keys.add(hx). Then sorted.
    keys = set(events.keys())
    for hx, _ in houses:
        keys.add(hx)
    # active horizontal counts
    active = {}  # y -> count
    ptr = 0
    ans = 0
    for k in sorted(keys):
        # apply events at k
        for delta, yy in events.get(k, ()):
            if delta == 1:
                active[yy] = active.get(yy,0)+1
            else:
                cnt = active[yy] - 1
                if cnt:
                    active[yy] = cnt
                else:
                    del active[yy]
        # query houses with x == k
        while ptr < N and houses[ptr][0] == k:
            hx, hy = houses[ptr]
            covered = hy in active
            if not covered:
                v = vertical_merged.get(hx)
                if v is not None:
                    starts, ends = v
                    idx = bisect.bisect_right(starts, hy) - 1
                    if idx >= 0 and ends[idx] >= hy:
                        covered = True
            if covered:
                ans += 1
            ptr += 1
    # final position is x,y after loop
    print(x, y, ans)

data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
idx = 0

for _ in range(N):
    x = int(data[idx]); y = int(data[idx+1]); idx += 2
...
for _ in range(M):
    d = data[idx]; idx += 1
    c = int(data[idx]); idx += 1
...
del data

Let's write optimized code with adds/removes.

Detailed code:

import sys
from collections import defaultdict
from bisect import bisect_right

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    idx = 0
    N = int(data[idx]); M = int(data[idx+1]); Sx = int(data[idx+2]); Sy = int(data[idx+3])
    idx += 4
    houses = []
    for _ in range(N):
        x = int(data[idx]); y = int(data[idx+1]); idx += 2
        houses.append((x, y))
    adds = defaultdict(list)
    removes = defaultdict(list)
    vertical = defaultdict(list)
    x = Sx; y = Sy
    for _ in range(M):
        d = data[idx]; idx += 1
        c = int(data[idx]); idx += 1
        if d == b'U':
            y2 = y + c
            vertical[x].append((y, y2))
            y = y2
        elif d == b'D':
            y2 = y - c
            vertical[x].append((y2, y))
            y = y2
        elif d == b'L':
            x2 = x - c
            adds[x2].append(y)
            removes[x + 1].append(y)
            x = x2
        else:  # R
            x2 = x + c
            adds[x].append(y)
            removes[x2 + 1].append(y)
            x = x2
    del data

    # merge vertical intervals
    v_merged = {}
    for vx, intervals in vertical.items():
        intervals.sort()
        starts = []
        ends = []
        for l, r in intervals:
            if not starts or l > ends[-1]:
                starts.append(l)
                ends.append(r)
            elif r > ends[-1]:
                ends[-1] = r
        v_merged[vx] = (starts, ends)
    # optional del vertical? We can del vertical to free memory before sorting keys? Yes after v_merged built, vertical no longer needed. del vertical.
    del vertical

    houses.sort(key=lambda p: p[0])

    keys = set(adds.keys())
    keys.update(removes.keys())
    for hx, _ in houses:
        keys.add(hx)
    # Could del adds/removes? Need still use. No.

    active = {}
    ptr = 0
    ans = 0
    # local variables for speed
    bisect = bisect_right
    for k in sorted(keys):
        # removals first
        if k in removes:
            for yy in removes[k]:
                cnt = active[yy] - 1
                if cnt:
                    active[yy] = cnt
                else:
                    del active[yy]
        if k in adds:
            for yy in adds[k]:
                active[yy] = active.get(yy, 0) + 1
        while ptr < N and houses[ptr][0] == k:
            hx, hy = houses[ptr]
            if hy in active:
                ans += 1
            else:
                v = v_merged.get(hx)
                if v is not None:
                    starts, ends = v
                    j = bisect(starts, hy) - 1
                    if j >= 0 and ends[j] >= hy:
                        ans += 1
            ptr += 1

    print(x, y, ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check `if k in removes` and `if k in adds` for every key. We can use `removes.get(k)` to avoid double lookup? Fine. But `if k in removes` then `removes[k]` does two lookups. Use `lst = removes.get(k); if lst: ...`. But list could be empty? never empty. Use:
lst = removes.get(k)
if lst:
   ...
lst = adds.get(k)
if lst:
   ...
This is efficient.

Need think about `active[yy]` in removal: If active count for yy exists. Should. But if using removes before adds, and there is a remove at x for a segment that ended at x-1; active count includes it. Good. If active[yy] missing due to bug, KeyError. Could use cnt = active.get(yy,0)-1; if cnt>0 else delete if 0? But if cnt<0, that would hide bug. Not needed. But to be safe? We can implement:
cnt = active.get(yy, 0) - 1
if cnt > 0: active[yy] = cnt
else:
    if cnt == 0:
        del active[yy]

Now, let's think about if there is a house at (x,y) and vertical intervals [0,2] and [3,4], house y=2.5 not integer; false. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a house at (x,y) and vertical intervals [0,2] and [3,4], house y=3.5 not integer; false. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a house at (x,y) and vertical intervals [0,2] and [3,4], house y=4.5 not integer;
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from collections import defaultdict
from bisect import bisect_right


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    idx = 0

    N = int(data[idx])
    M = int(data[idx + 1])
    Sx = int(data[idx + 2])
    Sy = int(data[idx + 3])
    idx += 4

    houses = []
    for _ in range(N):
        x = int(data[idx])
        y = int(data[idx + 1])
        idx += 2
        houses.append((x, y))

    adds = defaultdict(list)
    removes = defaultdict(list)
    vertical = defaultdict(list)

    x, y = Sx, Sy

    for _ in range(M):
        d = data[idx]
        idx += 1
        c = int(data[idx])
        idx += 1

        if d == b'U':
            y2 = y + c
            vertical[x].append((y, y2))
            y = y2
        elif d == b'D':
            y2 = y - c
            vertical[x].append((y2, y))
            y = y2
        elif d == b'L':
            x2 = x - c
            adds[x2].append(y)
            removes[x + 1].append(y)
            x = x2
        else:  # R
            x2 = x + c
            adds[x].append(y)
            removes[x2 + 1].append(y)
            x = x2

    del data

    # Merge vertical intervals for each x-coordinate.
    v_merged = {}
    for vx, intervals in vertical.items():
        intervals.sort()
        starts = []
        ends = []
        for l, r in intervals:
            if not starts or l > ends[-1]:
                starts.append(l)
                ends.append(r)
            elif r > ends[-1]:
                ends[-1] = r
        v_merged[vx] = (starts, ends)

    del vertical

    houses.sort()

    keys = set(adds.keys())
    keys.update(removes.keys())
    for hx, _ in houses:
        keys.add(hx)

    active = {}
    ptr = 0
    ans = 0

    for k in sorted(keys):
        lst = removes.get(k)
        if lst:
            for yy in lst:
                cnt = active[yy] - 1
                if cnt:
                    active[yy] = cnt
                else:
                    del active[yy]

        lst = adds.get(k)
        if lst:
            for yy in lst:
                active[yy] = active.get(yy, 0) + 1

        while ptr < N and houses[ptr][0] == k:
            hx, hy = houses[ptr]

            if hy in active:
                ans += 1
            else:
                v = v_merged.get(hx)
                if v is not None:
                    starts, ends = v
                    j = bisect_right(starts, hy) - 1
                    if j >= 0 and ends[j] >= hy:
                        ans += 1

            ptr += 1

    print(x, y, ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
